## Document 060: 2026年10月进出口数据点评：政策预期趋稳，外贸韧性有望延续

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月进出口数据点评：政策预期趋稳，外贸韧性有望延续.pdf
Report source: 中航证券
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告 中航证券研究所发布 
股市有风险   入市需谨慎

[Table_summary] 报告摘要 
◆ 进口显著提速，前10个月外贸增长稳中有增 
以美元计价，10月我国进出口同比+37.90%，其中出口+35.20%、进
口+41.60%，均超 Wind一致预期，单月规模均创历史同期新高，进出口
和进口增速均创年内新高，出口增速较 9月小幅回落但仍处高位；前 10
个月出口、进口累计分别+22.70%、+29.00%，均为 2022年以来同期最
高。出口方面，机电产品拉动+28.75PCTs，较 9月进一步抬升，集成电
路、自动数据处理设备及其零部件、汽车仍是主要拉动，但集成电路和
汽车拉动边际回落；当月八类重点商品量价齐升，范围较 9 月有所收
窄。进口方面，机电产品拉动+23.47PCTs，其中集成电路、自动数据处
理设备及其零部件分别拉动+13.64PCTs、+8.04PCTs，原油拉动明显增
强，进口支撑进一步扩展至能源产品。 
分目的地看，10月东盟、中国香港、美国位列当月拉动前三；前 10
个月东盟、中国香港、欧盟位列累计拉动前三，出口目的地结构总体稳
定，市场多元化格局保持稳定。外部景气看，10月有 7个制造业 PMI处
于扩张区间，较 9月减少 1个，外需仍有一定支撑，但扩张面有所收
窄，区域分化仍在延续。 
◆ 政策预期趋稳，全年外贸有望保持韧性 
10月，我国外贸运行呈现出口在高位小幅回落、进口增速明显提升
的态势。整体来看，10月我国外贸主要呈现以下特征：一是外贸总量延
续高位运行，进口提速对冲了出口增速的小幅回落；二是机电产品仍是
双向贸易的重要支撑，但出口核心品类和量价表现趋于分化，进口支撑
进一步扩展至能源品；三是出口目的地结构总体稳定，外部景气扩张面
收窄与政策预期改善并存。 
政策层面，中美举行第八轮经贸磋商，双方同意将吉隆坡经贸磋商
联合安排延期至 2027年 1月 10日，部分关税和出口管制措施继续暂
停，有助于延长政策缓冲期、稳定企业预期；同期，双方在贸易理事会
框架下就各自 300亿美元规模的对等降税安排达成共识，为相关商品贸
易改善提供了政策条件。11月 9日，商务部、海关总署进一步明确相关
出口管制措施自 11月 11日起继续暂停至 2027年 1月 10日，年内外贸
政策环境的可预期性进一步增强。整体来看，近期经贸安排以稳定存量
政策环境和管控分歧为主，政策预期趋稳或对年内外贸形成边际支撑。 
展望年内，虽然主要经济体制造业景气扩张面有所收窄，外需动能
或存在波动风险，但我国电子产业链相关贸易仍将维持活跃态势，外贸
核心支撑基本面未发生改变；进口增长的拉动来源进一步拓宽，出口市
场结构总体保持稳定，政策预期边际趋稳有望继续降低外贸企业经营不
确定性。整体来看，我国年内外贸仍具备保持韧性的基础，全年外贸以
较高增速收官的基础仍在，但年内后续出口或呈高位波动格局；进口在
电子零部件、能源品的支撑下有望维持较快增长。 
风险提示：全球经济超预期走弱；地缘政治事件烈度持续升级

一、进口显著提速，前 10个月外贸增长稳中有增 
以美元计价，10月我国进出口金额 7193.83亿美元，同比+37.90%。其中，出口
同比+35.20%，高于 Wind一致预期的+33.25%；进口同比+41.60%，高于 Wind一致预期
的+30.36%，分别高出预期 1.95个和 11.24个 PCTs。前 10个月，我国进出口金额
65229.29 亿美元，同比+25.30%，其中出口同比+22.70%，进口同比+29.00%。单月来
看，10月进出口和进口同比增速较 9月分别上升 3.80个和 10.50个 PCTs，均创年内
新高；出口增速较 9月回落 1.10个 PCTs，但仍处较高水平。累计来看，前 10个月进
出口、出口和进口增速均为 2022年以来同期最高，外贸增长呈现出稳中有增的态势。 
以美元计价，10月贸易差额 1052.77亿美元，较去年同期的 879.98亿美元增加
172.79亿美元，同比+19.64%；前 10个月累计贸易差额 10425.94亿美元，较去年同
期的 9583.73亿美元增加 842.21亿美元，同比+8.79%。以人民币计价，前 10个月贸
易差额 71632.65亿元，较去年同期的 68738.84亿元增加 2893.80亿元，同比+4.21%。 
图表 1：以美元计价进出口金额当月同比数据(%)

资料来源：wind，中航证券研究所 
10月出口产品结构延续机电产品主导的特征，但核心品类拉动有所分化。具体来
看，2026年 10月机电产品对出口同比增速的拉动为+28.75PCTs，较 9月的+27.63PCTs
上升 1.12个 PCTs，仍是出口增长的主要支撑（当月拉动=（该项目当月出口金额-该
项目去年同期出口金额）/去年同期总出口金额）。其中，集成电路、自动数据处理设
备及其零部件、汽车和汽车底盘的拉动分别为+7.51PCTs、+6.12PCTs和+1.77PCTs，较
9月分别回落 0.39个 PCTs、上升 0.98个 PCTs和回落 0.65个 PCTs，自动数据处理设
备及其零部件的拉动力的提升在一定程度上对冲了另外两类商品拉动的回落。从新型
产业看，高新技术产品对出口同比增速的拉动为+18.15PCTs，较 9月上升 0.72个 PCTs。
传统产业中，水产品的拉动为+0.04PCTs；纺织纱线、织物及其制品的拉动为+0.56PCTs，
较 9月回落 0.14个 PCTs。整体来看，上述传统商品对出口增速的拉动总体较为有限，
本月出口增长仍主要由机电产品支撑。 
进口方面，10月机电产品对进口同比增速的拉动为+23.47PCTs，较 9月上升 1.82
个 PCTs，仍是进口增长的首要支撑。其中，集成电路和自动数据处理设备及其零部件
的拉动力分别为+13.64PCTs和+8.04PCTs，分别较 9月上升 0.83个 PCTs和-0.91个
PCTs。此外，原油对进口的拉动由9月的+0.33PCTs升至+4.58PCTs，上升4.25个PCTs，
从量价数据看，10月原油进口价格同比涨幅较 9月扩大，进口数量同比降幅收窄，共
同推动原油进口金额同比增速明显回升。机电产品在我国进、出口两端的拉动同步上

升，一定程度上反映我国电子信息产业链相关贸易仍较活跃；同时，原油拉动明显增
强，10月进口支撑由电子产业链进一步扩展至能源产品。 
从出口量价关系看，10月成品油、稀土、箱包及类似容器、鞋靴、钢材、未锻造
的铝及铝材、家用电器、汽车和汽车底盘八类重点商品实现量价齐升，较 9月减少两
类。从重点跟踪商品看，集成电路出口价格同比涨幅最大，为+143.18%；汽车和汽车
底盘出口数量同比涨幅最大，为+33.53%。前 10个月累计来看，箱包及类似容器、未
锻造的铝及铝材、家用电器、集成电路、汽车和汽车底盘五类商品实现量价齐升，其
中集成电路出口价格同比+104.43%，汽车和汽车底盘出口数量同比+49.50%。10 月量
价齐升商品范围较 9月有所收窄，部分商品价格上升并未伴随数量同步增长，商品出
口增长的价格与数量贡献仍有明显分化。 
分出口目的地看，10月我国对东盟、中国香港和美国出口的拉动分别为+8.10PCTs、
+4.90PCTs和+3.77PCTs，位列当月前三，欧盟和韩国分别以+3.50PCTs和+3.27PCTs
位列第四、第五。拉动力变化情况来看，10 月各出口对象对出口同比增速的拉动力
整体波动较小，市场结构与 9月总体接近。在我们跟踪的 15个出口目的地中，14个
目的地的拉动较 9月变动幅度不超过 0.60个 PCTs，变动幅度最大的出口目的地为韩
国，拉动较 9月上升 0.72个 PCTs。10月我国对东盟、欧盟、中国香港、美国和拉丁
美洲五大市场的出口占比分别为 18.91%、13.24%、11.08%、11.26%和 7.73%。前 10
个月累计来看，我国对东盟、中国香港和欧盟出口的累计拉动分别为+5.35PCTs、
+4.35PCTs和+2.57PCTs，位列累计前三，韩国和非洲分别以+1.68PCTs和+1.40PCTs
位列第四、第五；对美出口的累计拉动升至+1.38PCTs，延续 6月转正以来的修复态
势。从出口结构占比看，前 10个月我国对东盟、欧盟、中国香港、美国和拉丁美洲
五大市场的出口占比分别为 18.66%、14.28%、10.67%、10.43%和 7.57%；与 2025年
全年相比，对东盟、中国香港出口占比分别上升 1.02个和 1.76个 PCTs，对美国、
欧盟和拉丁美洲出口占比分别回落 0.71个、0.58个和 0.32个 PCTs，中国香港出口
占比继续高于美国。整体来看，10 月出口目的地格局未出现明显切换，东盟和中国
香港仍是主要支撑，对美出口累计拉动继续修复，市场多元化格局总体保持稳定。 
从外部景气度看，我们监测的 10个主要经济体中（欧元区、日本、韩国、美国、
加拿大、俄罗斯、巴西、印度、南非和东盟，东盟作为整体看待），10月有 7个制造
业 PMI处于扩张区间，较 9月减少 1个。处于扩张区间的经济体中，美国 ISM制造业
PMI录得 55.5，较 9月回升 1.0个点；南非录得 55.6，较 9月回升 3.6个点；东盟
录得 53.1，较 9月回升 1.0个点。欧元区、日本、韩国和印度仍处扩张区间，但分
别录得 52.7、53.3、51.3和 54.7，较 9月分别回落 0.2个、0.8个、2.6个和 0.4
个点。加拿大录得 49.6，较 9月回落 1.9个点，由扩张区间转入收缩区间；俄罗斯
和巴西分别录得 49.9和 46.1，虽较 9月分别回升 0.1个和 1.3个点，但仍处收缩区
间。整体来看，主要经济体制造业景气仍有一定支撑，但扩张面有所收窄，外需动能
的区域分化仍在延续。

### Page 7

中航证券研究所发布 
二、政策预期趋稳，外贸韧性有望延续 
10月，我国外贸运行呈现出口在高位小幅回落、进口增速明显提升的态势。整体
来看，10 月我国外贸主要呈现以下特征：一是外贸总量延续高位运行，进口提速对
冲了出口增速的小幅回落；二是机电产品仍是双向贸易的重要支撑，但出口核心品类
和量价表现趋于分化，进口支撑进一步扩展至能源品；三是出口目的地结构总体稳定，
外部景气扩张面收窄与政策预期改善并存。 
政策层面，9月 20日至 23日，中美举行第八轮经贸磋商；据商务部 9月 24日介
绍，双方同意将吉隆坡经贸磋商联合安排延期至 2027年 1月 10日，部分关税和出
口管制措施继续暂停，有助于延长政策缓冲期、稳定企业预期；同期，双方在贸易理
事会框架下就各自 300 亿美元规模的对等降税安排达成共识，为相关商品贸易改善
提供了政策条件。11月 9日，商务部、海关总署进一步明确相关出口管制措施自 11
月11日起继续暂停至 2027年 1月 10日，年内外贸政策环境的可预期性进一步增强。
整体来看，近期经贸安排以稳定存量政策环境和管控分歧为主，政策预期趋稳或对年
内外贸形成边际支撑。 
展望年内，虽然主要经济体制造业景气扩张面有所收窄，外需动能或存在波动风
险，但我国电子产业链相关贸易仍将维持活跃态势，外贸核心支撑基本面未发生改变；
进口增长的拉动来源进一步拓宽，出口市场结构总体保持稳定，政策预期边际趋稳有
望继续降低外贸企业经营不确定性。整体来看，我国年内外贸仍具备保持韧性的基础，
全年外贸以较高增速收官的基础仍在，但年内后续出口或呈高位波动格局；进口在电
子零部件、能源品的支撑下有望维持较快增长。

### Page 8

中航证券研究所发布

公司的投资评级如下： 
买入：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数涨幅10%以上。 
增持：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数涨幅5%~10%之间。 
持有：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数涨幅-10%~+5%之间。 
卖出：未来六个月的投资收益相对沪深300指数跌幅10%以上。

行业的投资评级如下： 
增持：未来六个月行业增长水平高于同期沪深300指数。 
中性：未来六个月行业增长水平与同期沪深300指数相若。 
减持：未来六个月行业增长水平低于同期沪深300指数。

研究团队介绍汇总：  
中航证券宏观团队：立足国内，放眼国际，全面覆盖国内外宏观经济、政策研究。以自上而下的宏观视角，诠释经济运行趋势与规律，以把握流动性变化为
核心，指导大类资产配置。

在不与，未来也将不会与本报告中的具体推荐或观点直接或间接相关。

Greater China Technology Semiconductors
Asia Pacific
Industry View Attractive
Our recent downgrades have generated investor pushback. We
acknowledge that demand remains strong, but believe exposure
should be reduced before cloud-capex deceleration becomes

visible.
Following Cloud Semis: Peak Growth, Less Upside  and Old Memory: Peak Pricing
Power?, we address the main investor questions below.
1. Are the cuts to Montage’s earnings forecasts justified?
We acknowledge that our revised 2027 RCD shipment assumptions may prove too
conservative. We would characterize the shipment and revenue revisions as
cautious, incorporating uncertainty around procurement timing, platform transitions
and supply-chain execution. These assumptions leave room for upside if customer

orders and deployment proceed more smoothly than we currently allow for.
Structural content growth remains supportive, and sustained order strength would
warrant revisiting the degree of conservatism in our estimates.
2. What justifies the multiple compression (across memory and cloud semis),
and where is the catalyst?
Our central consideration is to reduce exposure before the cloud-capex deceleration
becomes visible. Valuations can adjust as investors reassess the durability of

growth, even while earnings continue to rise. Peaking earnings-revision breadth and
weaker visibility into 2028 server production reduce our confidence in extrapolating
recent growth rates.
For old memory, the concern is also around the sustainability of pricing and returns
as shortages narrow. Limited fixed-price LTAs enabled rapid price increases during
the upcycle, but provide less protection when demand weakens. Our updated
supply-demand models point toward normalization into 2028, supporting lower

valuation multiples before peak profitability rolls over.
The potential catalysts are softer order momentum and slower price increases. Such
timing remains uncertain, but we believe waiting for these developments to become
fully visible risks missing the valuation adjustment. Faster deployment, renewed
earnings upgrades and sustained capex acceleration beyond 1Q27 would challenge
our cautious view. (Continued)
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
November 15, 2026 03:36 PM GMT

### Page 2

M
 Idea
2

3. Have we changed our server or CPU shipment forecasts? If demand remains strong, 
why act now?
We have made no such industry-wide revisions. The hyperscalers’ 3Q results confirmed 
strong current spending. However, following the US midterms, our US team shifted 
approximately 5GW of expected US datacenter capacity additions from 2028 into 2029, 
equivalent to around 20% of previously expected 2028 additions (see Buy the Drop: AI 
Investment Opportunities Post the US Midterms). Our US team's hyperscaler capex 
forecasts remained unchanged at US$1.6trn and US$1.8trn for 2027 and 2028. This 
creates greater uncertainty around the timing of equipment procurement and deployment 
even with spending intentions intact.
We believe the combination of the completed 3Q earnings season and greater post-
election execution uncertainty provides a reasonable point to incorporate more risk. 
Current demand can remain strong while the balance of potential earnings revisions and 
valuation outcomes becomes less attractive.
4. Why retain Overweight on CXMT and Nanya Tech?
We differentiate by product mix, technology migration and valuation. Niche memory has a 
smaller supply base, more volatile pricing and generally less direct exposure to AI 
demand. Its pricing can respond sharply to relatively modest changes in supply, customer 
inventories and consumer demand. These characteristics make recent scarcity-driven 
profitability more vulnerable as supply catches up.
CXMT and Nanya also face memory-cycle risk. However, CXMT has additional growth 
drivers from capacity expansion, localization and process tech migration. We also continue 
to see tight DDR5 supply, with CXMT management indicating that customers had reserved 
2027 capacity for relevant enterprise products. For Nanya, stronger process technology 
and a clearer DDR5 migration path differentiate it from other DDR4 suppliers in our 
coverage, with DDR5 already contributing approximately 10% of its revenue.
Aspeed and Montage have concentrated exposure to server deployment and the cloud 
investment cycle. Their content-growth opportunities remain intact, but their earnings 
trajectories and valuations are particularly sensitive to changes in deployment timing and 
expectations for cloud-spending growth. We therefore see a stronger case for reducing 
exposure ahead of visible deceleration.

12-18 months.

the next 12-18 months.

universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

12-18 months.

Allen Chang 
+852-2978-2930 | 
allen.k.chang@gs.com

Verena Jeng 
+852-2978-1681 | verena.jeng@gs.com 
Goldman Sachs (Asia) L.L.C.
Aspeed (5274.TWO): Mgmt. visit: BMC and SMC specification migration 
and customers’ expansion to drive growth ahead; Buy
16 November 2026 | 11:05PM HKT
We visited Aspeed in Taipei recently. Management remain positive on future growth, 
riding on further customer expansion, product mix upgrade, and their continuous 
new product launches. The company highlights its comprehensive coverage across 
various chipset platforms (e.g. GPU, ASIC, RISC-V, etc.) and coverage across different 
layers in AI data center (e.g. component-level, server-level, rack-level, data 
center-level), securing their leading market position. We remain positive on Aspeed 
and see the growing servers demand along with AI servers design getting more 
complicated to support the company’s growth ahead. Maintain Buy. Read more: 
Aspeed Oct revenues and 3Q26 results in line. 
Key takeaways 
1. Positive tone on future growth: Management remain positive on future business 
growth, driven by further customer expansion, product mix upgrade, and their 
continuous new product launches. The company’s shipment is mainly from BMC2600 
in 2026, and 1700 and 2700 would further expand shipment in 2027, carrying higher 
ASP as stronger specification (e.g. integrated more functions, I/O supporting DDR5). 
The company would also have 2705 prior to 2800, which offers stronger security. The 
2800 is targeted to start sampling in 2028, and would be a driver beyond 2028, 
integrating more functions, stronger security (e.g. silicon-level security), stronger I/O, 
and using more advanced processing nodes. On SMC and Platform, the company will 
also have new products entering mass production in 2H27: (1) SMC 1040 and 1840 
(embedded FPGA, serving both single-node and multi-node), and (2) platform 1080, 
aiming to further expand customers. The company’s pure exposure to the server 
market also brings it strong end market demand, with the ability to pass through the 
higher raw material costs to end customers (e.g. higher costs from substrate, OSAT, 
foundry), supporting its GM. 
2. Competition advantages: Management highlight they are early in the market and 
serve various customers (e.g. GPU, ASIC, RISC-V, etc.), with comprehensive product 
coverage across different SoC platforms, and the company is also expanding its 
product portfolio vertically: across SMC (component-level mgmt.), BMC 
(server-level), and RMC (rack-level). The company’s products can monitor various 
scenarios, such as GPU thermal issue, liquid cooling leakage, etc. Management also 
highlight their large R&D team (over 100 engineers), local and timely support (having 
R&D team in Taiwan, close to customers), and ecosystem (work closely with ODMs). 
We remain positive on Aspeed’s leading market position and see AI server 
specifications getting more and more complicated (e.g. more shelves, more chips per

### Page 2

rack, from air cooling to liquid cooling, etc.), which would further enhance Aspeed’s 
competition edges. 
Price Target Risks and Methodology - Aspeed (5274.TWO) 
Valuation: Our 12-month TP of NT$22,000 is based on a target P/E of 40x (in line with 
its 10-year historical trading average) applied to our 2028E EPS, and discounted back to 
2027E at 13.6% CoE. Our key assumptions for CoE include: (1) 1.5x beta (sourcing 
Bloomberg), (2) a 4.25% risk-free rate, and (3) a market risk premium at 6.25% (in line 
with GS house view). 
Key downside risks to our view and TP: 1) A softer-than-expected server market 
demand recovery, 2) slower-than-expected BMC penetration within AI servers, 3) 
intensifying market competition.

Source: Company data, Goldman Sachs Research estimates, FactSet. Price as of 16 Nov 2026 close.

壹、訪 談／法 說 內 容 
重點摘要： 
 資料中心新專案自 3Q26 開始放量，3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ 
+28.8%、YoY +39.6%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元；10 月營收進一步
提高至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，本研究預估
4Q26 營收 3.97 億元，下半年營運動能明顯高於上半年。 
 2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估台達電集團訂單
營收占比由 2025 年的 41.5%提升至 2026 年約 50%、2027 年
約 65%，成為未來兩年主要營收增量來源。 
 受惠新訂單放量，本研究預 2026／2027 年營收分別為 11.8 億
元／17.8 億元，2027 年 YOY+50.8%，新增業務以台達電相關
OEM 線束為主，毛利率將維持一定區間較難提升，預估 2026
／2027 年毛利率 23.9%／23.4%，獲利成長主要來自營收放
量，稅後 EPS 分別為 1.61／3.31 元。

一、2H26 新專案開始放量，營收動能明顯轉強 
3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，毛利率 24.43%，
較 2Q26 維持前期水準；營業利益 2,993 萬元，營業利益率 9.17%，
較 2Q26 的 7.43%提高 1.74 個百分點，稅後淨利 2,156 萬元，EPS 
0.55 元。累計前三季營收 7.78 億元、EPS 0.90 元，營運較 2025
年同期明顯改善。

受惠資料中心新專案持續放量，7／8／9 月營收分別為 9,788 萬元
／1.05 億元／1.24 億元，呈現逐月成長。10 月營收進一步提升至
1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，創單月新高。本研究依
10 月實際營收、既有業務營運水準及新專案出貨進度推估，4Q26
營收約 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8% ，毛利率 23.65%，營業利益率
11.1%，稅後 EPS 約 0.71 元，下半年營運成長動能持續增強。

二、AI 電力需求成長，新專案放量，2027 年全年貢獻 
AI 算力需求提升帶動資料中心電力密度增加，電源架構朝高功
率、高電流及高可靠度方向發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及
PSU 等電源與備援系統需求同步增加。映興產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中
心機櫃、備援電力模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，
公司產品亦可延伸至大電流與高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

三、既有應用維持成長，較高毛利產品支撐產品組合 
除 AI 資料中心外，公司既有產品橫跨工業應用、移動載具、車載
電子及儲能等領域。移動載具涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩
托車、滑板車及船外機等所需的防水連接器與線束模組；車載電
子則供應車用風扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；工業應用包括氫燃料
電池、馬達、機械手臂與家電相關線束及代理元件。

公司自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具微型大電流、高插
拔次數、IP68 防水及抗火花等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主運算
工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、IATF 
16949 及 UL 等認證。

本研究預期車載及移動載具等較高毛利產品持續成長，可部分抵
銷 AI 資料中心新增 OEM 線束占比提高造成的毛利率負向影響。
3Q26 實際毛利率仍達 24.43%，顯示新增訂單放量尚未造成毛利率
明顯下降；惟隨 2027 年 OEM 營收占比進一步提高，產品組合仍
有毛利率些許負向影響。因此映興未來兩年營運型態將呈現「營
收快速成長、毛利率大致維持、費用率下降」，獲利成長主要來
自營收規模放大，而非毛利率擴張。

四、預估 2026／2027 年稅後 EPS 1.61／3.31 元 
2026 年受惠資料中心新專案自 2H26 開始貢獻，本研究預估全年
營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5% ；毛利率 23.9%、營業費用約 1.82
億元，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%降至 15.5%，帶動營業利益約 9,837
萬元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%，EPS 1.61 元。

2027 年新專案進入完整年度貢獻，本研究預估全年營收 17.75 億
元，YoY +51.1%；毛利率 23.4%、營業費用約 2.20 億元，費用率
進一步降至 12.4%，營業利益約 1.95 億元、營業利益率 11.0%。
預估稅後淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.31 元，YoY 約+106%。 
五、投資建議：買進，目標價 59 元 
隨台達電訂單新專案貢獻，映興有新的營運動能，帶動營收與 EPS
正向成長，以 2027 年 EPS3.31 計算，根據歷史區間與同業評價，
給予 18 倍本益比，目標價 59 元

貳、產 業 及 營 運 概 況 
一、公司簡介 
映興電子成立於 1986 年，主要從事連接線束模組、代理元件及自
有防水連接器，營運據點涵蓋台灣、中國深圳與昆山、泰國及美
國。公司產品可分為三類：第一為 OEM／ODM／OBM 連接線束
模組；第二為代理元件，包括 Sensata 溫度保護元件、日本 Kurabe
特殊電線及 Anderson Power 大電流連接器；第三為自有防水連接
器。公司目前聚焦 AI 伺服器、工業應用、移動載具、車載電子及
綠能儲能五大應用市場。

公司核心能力在於複雜線束加工、防水連接器設計及代理元件整
合。自有防水連接器累積 93 項國內外專利，具備 IP68 防水、抗
火花、高插拔次數及微型大電流等特性；OEM 線束則可透過自主
運算工具進行阻抗與結構優化設計，並取得 ISO 9001、ISO 14001、
IATF 16949 及 UL 等認證。相較一般單純線材加工廠，公司可利
用一線品牌代理權作為客戶導入入口，再延伸至線束加工與自有
連接器，提高單一客戶合作深度。

五大應用中，AI 伺服器主要供應資料中心 HVDC／PDU／CDU 電
源機櫃、UPS／BBU 備援電池櫃、TPS 基地台機房及 PSU 內部線
束；工業應用涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬達、機械手臂及家電所需線束
與代理元件；移動載具包括無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、
滑板車及船外機所需防水連接器與線束；車載電子則供應車用風
扇、OBC 及煞車系統線束；綠能儲能則以太陽能儲能、大電流連
接器及電池櫃線束為主，相關技術亦可延伸至 AI 資料中心 BBU
備援電力應用。

二、AI 新專案放量，台達電集團訂單營收占比持續提升 
AI 資料中心為映興目前最重要的新增成長來源。隨 AI 算力需求
提升，資料中心電力系統逐步朝高功率、高電流與高可靠度方向
發展，HVDC、PDU、UPS／BBU 及 PSU 等電源與備援系統需求
同步增加。公司目前相關產品已涵蓋 AI 資料中心機櫃、備援電力
模組、基地台機房及電源供應系統等線束模組，因此可隨下游資
料中心電源系統需求擴張而受惠。公司亦已具備 UPS 電池系統、
IT Rack 、PDU 及電池模組串接等產品經驗，可延伸至 AI 資料中
心大電流及高可靠度電力傳輸應用。

公司並非直接供應 CSP，而是透過台達電集團訂單切入終端雲端
服務業者供應鏈。映興供應內容集中於資料中心電源系統內部線
束與連接元件，因此營運節奏與台達電集團資料中心電源業務高
度連動。除了資料中心電源機櫃外，公司在台達電集團既有業務
亦涵蓋 PSU、車載充電系統、基地台機房、儲能櫃及自動化設備
等產品，客戶合作範圍並非僅限於 AI 資料中心單一應用。

本研究估算，2025 年台達電集團訂單營收占比約四成；2026 年上
半年新專案尚未正式進入主要營收貢獻期，因此相關營收仍以
TPS、車用及既有電源產品為主。隨資料中心新專案於 3Q26 開始
放量，7、8、9 月公司合併營收逐月提升，3Q26 營收達 3.26 億元，
QoQ +28.8%。10 月營收進一步增加至 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、
YoY +76.69%，本研究預期台達電集團訂單相關營收成長動能自下
半年起明顯轉強。

本研究對資料中心新專案採 Bottom-up 推估，將台達電集團訂單
營收區分為既有業務與資料中心新增業務，並依已公告營收、既
有業務正常營運水準及後續專案 Ramp 節奏進行推估，而非直接
以客戶占比反推公司營收。由於 2026 年新專案僅貢獻部分年度，

2027 年同時享有全年化與出貨增加兩項效果，本研究預估相關新
專案營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍，帶動台達電集團訂單營收占比由
2026 年約 50%進一步提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要
營收增量來源。

惟台達電集團訂單相關業務主要仍以 OEM 線束為主，毛利率相對
低於自有防水連接器、車載及部分移動載具產品，因此台達電集
團訂單營收占比快速提升將對產品組合形成一定毛利壓力。不
過，3Q26 毛利率仍達 24.43%，維持前期水準，加上集團既有產能
可因應新增訂單，營收規模放大有助於固定製造成本攤提。本研
究預估 2026／2027 年毛利率約 23.9%／23.4%，營業利益率則由
8.3%提高至 11.0%，顯示本輪成長主要來自 AI 帶動營收放量及費
用率下降，而非毛利率成長。 
三、車載與移動載具高毛利產品及其他業務正向發展 
移動載具產品涵蓋無人機、機器狗、自行車、摩托車、滑板車、
輪椅及船外機所需的防水連接器與線束模組。公司在此領域主要
競爭力來自自有防水連接器，可提供 IP68、防水、抗火花及高插
拔次數設計，適合戶外、震動及高濕度等環境，產品附加價值高
於一般 OEM 線束。公司原有兩輪載具為既有核心市場，近年亦逐
步向無人機與四足機器人等新應用延伸。

考量自行車庫存逐步正常化、無人機非紅供應鏈需求提高，以及
機器狗等新型移動載具應用擴張，本研究預估移動載具中短期營
收可維持中個位數成長，並以約 5～8%作為 2027 年相關業務成長
假設。由於此類產品較多採用公司自有防水連接器及客製化線
束，其毛利結構優於一般 OEM 產品，因此除營收成長外，亦可作
為未來產品組合的重要支撐。

車載電子方面，公司主要供應車用風扇、On-board Charger 及煞車
系統線束，已取得 IATF 16949 車用認證，可切入對品質、可靠度
及認證要求較高的汽車供應鏈。公司現有產品已涵蓋車載充電、
煞車系統及相關電力電子應用，隨全球汽車朝電動化與智慧化發
展，電力電子、充電系統及熱管理需求仍具中長期成長趨勢。

考量車用產品認證週期較長、導入後客戶黏著度相對較高，加上
電動車及高階車用電子滲透率持續提升，本研究預估車載電子中
長期營收成長約 10～15%。部分高階車用產品專案能見度亦延伸
至未來數年，預期車載業務可持續成為公司較穩定、且毛利率優
於一般 OEM 線束的產品線。

工業應用為公司既有營收的重要來源，產品涵蓋氫燃料電池、馬

達、機械手臂、燃油引擎所需線束模組與代理元件，以及空調壓
縮機、吹風機、吸塵器等家電使用的代理元件；客戶應用橫跨燃
料電池、工業自動化與消費型家電。

本研究預期工業應用仍受惠工業自動化、燃料電池及資料中心相
關電力需求，維持穩健成長。不過須注意公司應用別與客戶別分
類並非完全對應，部分 AI 資料中心及基礎建設相關產品亦可能歸
類於工業應用，因此本研究在財測時不直接套用單一工業應用成
長率，以避免與台達電集團訂單 AI 專案重複計算。

綠能儲能方面，公司產品包括太陽能儲能系統、大電流儲能連接
器及電池櫃串接線束，其技術與 AI 資料中心 BBU 在大電流傳輸、
電池模組串接及高可靠度連接等方面具有共通性。隨儲能與資料
中心備援電力需求增加，公司過去累積的電池櫃及大電流連接技
術可同時支援傳統儲能及 AI 資料中心相關應用，提供中長期新增
業務機會。

整體而言，本研究預期其他客戶業務將由車載、移動載具、工業
及儲能等既有應用共同支撐。2026 年在既有應用回升下，預估其
他客戶營收約 5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%；考量 2027 年基期提高，
且工業分類中部分 AI 營收已另於台達電集團訂單模型估算，本研
究採較保守成長假設，預估 2027 年其他客戶營收約 6.27 億元，
YoY 約+7%。此部分雖非未來兩年最大營收增量來源，但較高毛
利產品比重有助於抵銷 AI OEM 線束占比上升造成的產品組合壓
力。 
叁、業 績 及 獲 利 預 估 
一、預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 1.61 元 
本研究將營收拆分為「台達電集團訂單」與「其他客戶」兩部分。
台達電集團訂單再區分既有業務及資料中心新專案；其他客戶則
依車載、移動載具、工業及儲能等既有應用的實際營運水準與成
長趨勢估算。台達電集團訂單營收占比為兩部分個別預估後除算
得出，而非預先假設占比後回推營收。

3Q26 營收 3.26 億元，QoQ +28.8%、YoY +39.6%，較本研究原預
估 3.22 億元高約 1.5%；毛利率 24.43%，優於原預估 23.2%，惟營
業費用 4,976 萬元，亦高於原預估 4,200 萬元，營業利益 2,993 萬
元、營業利益率 9.17%，稅後 EPS 0.55 元。雖然營收與毛利率優
於原預估，但費用同步增加，顯示新增訂單放量後仍需考量營運
規模擴大所帶來的費用成長。

10 月營收 1.30 億元，MoM +5.03%、YoY +76.69%，亦高於本研究

公司研究報告

或個股之相關條件改變，投資人需自行考量投資之實際狀況與風險，本公司恕不負擔任何法律責任及做任何保

7 
原估約 1.23 億元。考量資料中心新專案出貨進度優於原先預期，
本研究上修 4Q26 營收至 3.97 億元，QoQ +21.8%。毛利率考量 3Q26
實際維持 24%以上，且產能利用率提高有助於固定製造成本攤
提，預估 4Q26 毛利率 24.0%；營業費用則依 3Q26 實際水準估約
5,000 萬元，營業利益約 4,534 萬元、營業利益率 11.4%，稅 後 EPS
約 0.73 元。下半年營收合計約 7.24 億元，較上半年增加約 60.3%。

本研究預估 2026 全年營收 11.75 億元，YoY +36.5%，其中台達電
集團訂單營收約 5.89 億元，占營收比重約 50%；其他客戶營收約
5.86 億元，YoY 約+16.5%，維持雙位數成長。

雖新增台達電集團訂單以 OEM 線束為主，產品組合對毛利率形成
一定的負向影響，但 3Q26 實際毛利率仍維持 24.43%，反映營收
規模增加可部分抵銷產品組合影響。本研究預估 2026 年毛利率約
23.9%，較 2025 年的 23.6%小幅提升。營業費用預估約 1.82 億元，
YoY 約+21.9%，增幅低於營收，費用率由 2025 年的 17.4%下降至
15.5%，帶動營業利益約 1.00 億元、營業利益率提升至 8.37%。

業外損益預估約-598 萬元，考量前三季實際稅率及 4Q26 有效稅率
35%的假設，預估全年有效稅率約 31.7%，稅後淨利約 6,404 萬元，
EPS 1.63 元。獲利成長主要來自營收放量、固定成本攤提改善及
營業費用率下降，而非單純依賴毛利率提升。

二、預估 2027 年稅後 EPS 3.31 元  
2027 年營收成長主要來自資料中心新專案。由於 2026 年新專案僅
自下半年開始貢獻，2027 年除完整貢獻全年外，出貨仍持續 Ramp 
up。本研究考量 3Q26 實際營收與 10 月營收均優，預估新專案相
關營收較 2026 年成長逾 2 倍；既有台達電集團訂單業務則維持穩
定，預估全年台達電集團訂單營收約 11.48 億元。

其他客戶方面，本研究預估 2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年考
量車載、移動載具及其他既有業務持續成長，但 2026 已有較高基
期，因此採較正常化成長假設，預估全年營收約 6.27 億元，YoY
約+7%。

兩者加總後，預估 2027 年營收 17.75 億元，YoY +51.1%，台達電
集團訂單營收占比提高至約 65%。相較原預估 16.94 億元，本次主
要反映資料中心新專案出貨進度提前，而非 ASP 上升。

季度營收預估呈逐季上升，1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 分別為 4.05
／4.29／4.58／4.83 億元。新專案出貨增加使台達電集團訂單營收
占比逐步提高，但考量 OEM 比重上升，毛利率維持；另一方面，

新增營收有助於產能利用率及固定成本攤提改善，本研究預估四
季毛利率分別為 23.55%／ 23.43%／ 23.35%／ 23.25%，全年約
23.39%。

營業費用方面，本研究依 3Q26 實際費用及 4Q26 預估費用水準進
行推估，考量營運規模擴大，預估 1Q27／2Q27／3Q27／4Q27 營
業費用分別為 5,200／5,400／5,600／5,820 萬元，全年約 2.20 億
元，YoY 約+20.8%；惟營收增幅高於費用，預估全年費用率由 2026
年的 15.5%降至 12.4%，帶動營業利益約 1.95 元、營業利益率
11.0%。

業外收入預估約 500 萬元，有效稅率維持 35%，預估 2027 年稅後
淨利約 1.31 億元，EPS 3.33 元，YoY 約+104%。季度 EPS 預估分
別為 0.74／0.80／0.87／0.93 元，營業利益率隨營收放量由 1Q27
的 10.7%逐步提高至 4Q27 的 11.3%，顯示本輪獲利成長仍主要由
營業槓桿帶動。 
三、台達電集團訂單為主要成長，其他業務維持穩健  
台達電集團訂單方面，本研究預估既有業務維持正常營運水準，
資料中心新專案則自 2H26 進入放量階段。考量 3Q26 實際營收及
10 月營收均優，預估 2026／2027 年台達電集團訂單營收分別為
5.89／11.48 億元，YoY 約+65%／+95%，占合併營收比重由 2026
年的約 50%提高至 2027 年約 65%，為未來兩年最主要營收增量來
源。

其他客戶方面，本研究依公司既有應用營運趨勢進行估算，預估
2026 年營收約 5.86 億元，2027 年增加至約 6.27 億元，YoY 約+7%。
2027 年增速較 2026 正常化，主要考量 2026 已有較高基期。

yangjway-72349@esunsec.com.tw

研 究 報 告                
大銀微系統(4576.TT) 
產業：電機機械 
受惠半導體設備擴充潮，2027 年營收成⻑將⾼於 20%  
 投資評價與建議 
高毛利率的奈微米定位平台營收比重已近60%，產品結構及附加價值提升帶動
毛利率提升，技術升級、集團分工與產能區域化佈局將帶動大銀微2027年營收
成⻑高於20%。 
 重點評論分析 
3Q26營收年增77%，4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略⾼：亞洲(台灣除外)、
台灣、美洲及歐洲地區3Q26分別年增89%、101%、127%及-12%，全球半導
體供應鏈重組與在地化採購需求強勁，3Q26營收12.2億元季增19%(年增
77%)，高階定位平台出貨佔比提升及規模經濟發揮，毛利率42.4%季增
5.3pp(年增8.6pp)，規模經濟及費用控制得宜下，營業利益率24.3%季增
6.4pp(年增13.9pp)；稅前淨利3.3億元季增113%(年增255%)，EPS 2.15元，
1~3Q26EPS 4.07元；4Q26營收可望與3Q26持平或略高，毛利率將維持在
40%上下。 
發展策略將持續朝向⾼附加價值市場，2027年營收成⻑將⾼於20%：未來的
發展策略將持續朝向高附加價值市場邁進，包括發展精密力量控制技術（涵蓋

銷售，不單獨販售E1控制器，以確保軟硬體深度綁定，帶動營收與獲利持續同
步雙位數成⻑，2027年營收成⻑高於20%。

季增6.4pp(年增13.9pp)；稅前淨利3.3億元季增113%(年增255%)，EPS 2.15元，1~3Q26EPS 4.07元；毛利率提升主
要是產品結構及附加價值提升，奈微米定位平台由於高度客製化、整合空氣軸承、花崗岩平台、多軸線性馬達與高階雷
射補償演算法，且直接對接半導體⼀線客戶，毛利率達40~50%；精密運動及控制元件過去單賣馬達線圈與磁軌毛利率
未及30%，但大銀微力推「馬達＋自製E1系列驅動器＋控制器」整套搭配銷售，加上高單價晶圓移載用直驅力矩馬達出
貨放量，使元件部門毛利率亦同步墊高至30~35%。

晶片尺寸逼近光罩極限，晶圓代工龍頭與OSAT廠加速推進FOPLP與矩陣式大面積封裝，大面積玻璃或有機基板在高速
取放、曝光與AOI檢測時，傳統滾珠螺桿會產生背隙與熱變形，必須全面改採「大跨距龍門雙驅無鐵芯線性馬達平台」
與「大型花崗岩空氣軸承系統」，大銀微在此領域具備完整大尺寸平台調校經驗，單機採用金額較傳統封裝設備提升2~3
倍；環繞式閘極與晶背供電大幅增加製程道數與光學/電子束檢測站點，歐美設備客戶為提升毛利率，持續將次系統委由
大銀微ODM，訂單能見度已直達2Q27；全球AI伺服器主板與交換器板廠在泰國大舉擴產，帶動高階雷射鑽孔機、機械
鑽孔機與成型機對大銀微線性馬達與高階驅動器的強勁拉貨；配合上銀(2049)集團佈局AI人型機器人，大銀微的高轉矩
密度無框力矩馬達與微型驅動器，具備切入機器人旋轉關節與靈巧⼿電控系統之⻑期潛力。 
CPO 相關營收訂單能見度已達 2H27：AI 資料中心算力互連由銅線轉向光學互連（800G/1.6T 光模組與 CPO），光
纖陣列（FAU）與矽光晶片波導進行主動對位（Active Alignment）時，要求定位精度必須達到奈米級且兼具極高反
應速度與抗震性，大銀微系統能解決高倍率顯微鏡檢測時的微小震動干擾，有效提升客戶設備產出率，主要應用於光
學引擎測試與系統級檢測，優勢在於可提供高精密、輕薄且體積緊湊的六軸定位平台，E1 控制器能將伺服穩定度提升
至正負 1 奈米，由於大銀的線性馬達是完全自行研發，因此可做到比業界體積更小，且可做到機電整合；大銀微開發
之多自由度奈米定位平台已切入台系與美系矽光子檢測及封裝設備鏈，2Q26 起開始交貨國內矽光子檢測設備大廠，
2H26 進⼀步放量，訂單能見度已達 2H27。 
2027 年資本支出為 1.2~1.5 億元，將新增 20~30%產能：面對全球半導體供應鏈區域化（美、日、歐晶圓廠擴建）
以及 AI 伺服器帶動高階 PCB/載板廠大舉移往泰國與東南亞設廠，大銀微採取「核心奈米平台留台灣台中擴產、標準
控制元件與次系統模組強化亞洲及歐美前線組裝」的雙軌策略，2H26 台中廠區針對高階微奈米定位系統與精密運動元
件已啟動擴產計畫，擴產產能規劃於 4Q26~1Q27 陸續完成，屆時將增加 20~30%的產能，進⼀步⽀撐 4Q26~1Q27
的出貨順暢度；第⼀階段產能擴建已完成，第二階段擴產預計於 2027 年 2 月底至 3 月中旬完成，並已開始規劃第三
階段，2027 年資本⽀出為 1.2~1.5 億元。

| 大銀微的定位與優勢 
大銀微掌握直驅馬達、線性馬達與驅動控制技術：大銀微系統成立於1997年，為上銀集團旗下的機電與超精密定位核心
企業，在集團分工體系中，⺟公司上銀（2049）負責提供高剛性機械結構與精密線性傳動部件，而大銀微則負責提供「大
腦（控制器）」、「神經（驅動器）」與「直接驅動心臟（線性馬達、力矩馬達、音圈馬達）」，大銀微與上銀為機電
整合雙引擎，大銀微掌握直驅馬達、線性馬達與驅動控制技術，而上銀專精於奈米與微米級的機械傳動（螺桿、線軌、
減速機）；大銀微的優勢包括馬達與驅動器與控制器「三合⼀」全自製能力(可針對奈米級平台進行底層演算法調校)、

### Page 4

4 
集團垂直整合綜效(結合上銀高精度螺桿/滑軌與自⾝線性馬達，提供⼀站式機電整合⽅案)、已通過全球前三大半導體設
備商及晶圓代工龍頭嚴苛認證。 
產業現狀：日本政府禁止高階工具機出口中國大陸，而中國大陸也管制高精度工具機出口，在全球貿易壁壘高築下，包
括北美、東南亞訂單大舉湧向台灣工具機業者；惟台灣工具機整機廠高階市場現面臨日本工具機廠挾日圓貶值優勢的威
脅，中低階或泛用型標準機又遭逢大陸業者低價搶市的威脅。 
產品應用範圍涵蓋半導體設備、面板及光電與自動化及PCB與載板設備等：大銀微的產品應用範圍涵蓋半導體設備(佔比
逾40%)、面板及光電與自動化（佔比約30~35%）、PCB與載板設備（佔比約20%）、精密量測及自動化、生技醫療等
產業，重量級客戶有應材（AMAT）、艾斯摩爾（ASML）、科磊（KLAC）及東京威力科創(8035.T)等全球前四大半導
體設備製造廠。

| 機器人產業簡介 
服務型機器人佔比較⾼，人形機器人正在起步：依據Precedence Research預估，服務型機器人市場2026年為725億美
元，2035年將可增加至2,338億美元，年均複合成⻑率為14%；工業型機器人市場2026年為307億美元，2035年將可增
加至933億美元，年均複合成⻑率為13%；人形機器人市場2026年為22億美元，2035年將可增加至88億美元，年均複
合成⻑率為35%，人形機器人雖然市場成⻑速度較快，但由於正在起步，市場規模及滲透率仍較低。 
機器人市場

資料來源 : Precedence Research、 玉山投顧整理、繪製 
人形機器人的結構與零組件

資料來源 : IEK、 玉山投顧整理、繪製

### Page 5

5 
AI模型讓機器人可持續學習優化：機器人智能可分為三個決策層、執行層及感知層三個主要層級，決策層負責環境理解、
任務規劃與行為決策；執行層由機器人本體與運動控制系統組成，包括伺服馬達、減速機、關節模組與控制器，負責把 
AI 的決策轉換成具體動作；感知層負責蒐集環境資訊，常見設備有RGB或RGB-D攝影機、雷射測距儀、力矩與觸覺感
測器，以及慣性量測單元，透過多模態感測資訊融合，系統才能對環境與物件有更精準的理解；AI模型讓機器人可持續
學習優化，隨著人形機器人與雙臂操作機器人快速發展，機構設計也愈來愈接近人類，如多自由度關節、靈巧⼿等設計，
讓機器人能在既有的人類工作環境中執行更複雜的任務。 
機器人智能系統主要層級包括決策層、執行層與感知層

資料來源 : 台達電 
控制器相當於機器人的大腦，AI時代更形重要：控制器的主要功能包括運動控制(計算多軸反向運動學，實現精準定位與
軌跡追蹤)、信號傳遞(將邏輯決策轉為電氣信號傳給驅動器與馬達)、安全防護(緊急停止與安全監控保護人員與設備)與
系統整合(連接視覺、力覺感測器以及工廠聯網通訊) ，AI時代將更形重要。 
AI時代控制器更形重要

資料來源 : 新代法說會簡報 
人機協作為目前工廠新典型，未來將走向智慧化：人機協作是指人類與人工智慧、機器人或自動化系統共同工作、互補
優勢以達成任務的模式，它透過機器處理大量數據與重複性工作，結合人類的創造力、同理心與終端決策，實現效率與
價值的最大化；核心優勢與分工在人機協作的模式下，人類與機器各司其職，發揮最強效能，機器擅⻑資料快速運算、
例行程序自動化、預測分析與24小時監控，而人類擅⻑戰略⽅向決策、複雜情境判斷、創意發想以及人際同理心溝通；
人機協作的共同效益為大幅降低人力閒置、提升組織生產力，並協助員工從低技術工作轉型為高附加價值工作，人機協
作為目前工廠新典型，但未來將走向智慧化，智慧化機器人結合了AI、先進感測器與精密控制系統，能自主感知周遭環
境、進行即時資料分析、做出決策，並執行原本需要人類智慧與物理動作才能完成的複雜任務，將AI實體化，其核心技

### Page 6

6 
術與特點在於將大腦演算法與實體機械結合，讓機器人具備空間理解與動態適應能力，並具有高精度感測與視覺，可透
過3D深度相機、光達與扭力感測器，做到精準避障與靈敏控制，由於具有模組化關節與驅動，透過高度整合馬達、驅動
器與通訊介面，提升扭力密度與即時反應速度；智慧化機器人主要應用領域智慧製造包括彈性產線、自動化組裝、設備
巡檢與物料搬運；醫療與照護⽅面如⽀援⼿術輔助、醫療物流與高齡化社會的照護陪伴；也能應用於特殊與極端環境，
如進入災害現場、地下空間或危險區域進行偵察與救援。 
協作型機器人市場2034年預估達713億美元

資料來源 : Precedence Research、 玉山投顧整理、繪製 
協作型機器人市場至2034年的年均複合成⻑率為35%：協作型機器人（Cobot）是⼀種專為與人類在共同工作空間內安
全協作而設計的機器人，與傳統工業機器人不同，其設計核心不是「最大化速度與力量」，而是「讓機器能夠安全地在
人的旁邊工作」，協作型機器人的概念最早在 1990 年代由學術界提出，2008年丹麥Universal Robots推出第⼀款商
業化Cobot協作型機器人後，這個品類才真正進入工業應用視野。此後十餘年間，隨著感測技術、控制演算法與製造成
本的成熟，協作型機器人快速普及，從歐美大廠擴展到亞洲製造業的中小型工廠。依據Precedence Research預估，協
作型機器人市場2025年為56億美元，2034年將可增加至713億美元，年均複合成⻑率為35%。

## Document 271: ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens

Source PDF: all report pdf/ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-20
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens
Date:    17 minutes ago
ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold Panic Worsens

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
ANT's IPO Is Hot, But Stock
Market Gets Cold Feet And Gold
Panic Worsens
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 19 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gary Meulemans / Unsplash

I. Anthropic Makes History, Stocks Fall
Today marked Anthropic's stock market debut. It showed that enthusiasm
for AI remains strong, even as broader market sentiment has soured.
Anthropic raised $81.3 billion through its IPO, surpassing SpaceX's $75
billion offering to become the largest IPO in history. Including a concurrent

### Page 2

private placement, the deal raised $107.3 billion. The shares were priced
at $83, valuing Anthropic at $1.5 trillion and making it the world's 12th
most valuable publicly traded company. They opened at $111.50, briefly
pushing its valuation above $2 trillion, before retreating from an intraday
high of $115.10 to $95.05, still 14.5% above the IPO price.
The S&P 500 reversed an early rally this morning to fall 2.0%, while the
Nasdaq dropped 2.2% (charts). Not even the largest IPO in history could

reverse the jitters surrounding the AI trade.
The selloff extended to semiconductor stocks, with AMD plunging 7.7%
after a short-seller report raised concerns about its ties to Chinese
entities, including a US-blacklisted server manufacturer (chart). Coming
on the back of the Nvidia controversy and tighter US export controls, the

### Page 3

report provided another reminder of the political speed bumps facing the
AI trade.
For us, however, the IPO's biggest significance lies ahead. We have
argued that financial, political, and physical constraints may slow the AI
buildout, but are unlikely to halt it as long as demand for compute keeps
growing. Anthropic's future earnings reports will provide a clearer window
into AI adoption across the economy, helping us assess whether demand
for compute is strong enough to sustain one of the US economy's most

important stimulus programs: the AI buildout. We believe it is and continue
to view the recent selloff as a buying opportunity.
We are sticking with our S&P 500 target of 7,900, based on projected
2027 EPS of $425 and a forward P/E of 18.6. Industry analysts continue
to raise earnings estimates, with the consensus 2028 EPS estimate at
$496 versus our $450 forecast (chart). We suspect some irrational
exuberance, as these forecasts fail to account for the AI buildout's speed

bumps.

### Page 4

Investors, meanwhile, are increasingly skeptical of the analysts' optimistic
forecasts. So investors pushed the S&P 500 forward P/E down to 17.6
(chart). That's below last year's Liberation Day low. We think that panic
selling should abate soon.
Today's latest stock market selloff suggests that the two Bull/Bear Ratios
we track will continue to fall below their averages over time (since 1987)
this week (chart). From a contrarian perspective, that would be bullish.

### Page 5

II. US Capital Flows Supporting The Dollar
The latest Treasury International Capital (TIC) data show that foreign
demand for US financial assets remained exceptionally strong through
September. Net foreign capital inflows totaled $1.43 trillion over the 12
months through September, remaining near the record-high $1.82 trillion
last year (chart).
Equities attracted a staggering $944.7 billion, including $787.1 billion from
private foreign investors and $157.6 billion from foreign official institutions

(chart).

### Page 6

The 12-month total for foreign equity inflows has surged over the past two
years to around $1.0 trillion on a 12-month basis (chart).
Private foreign purchases of US equities reached $787.1 billion, nearly
matching the $821.5 billion pouring into US bonds (chart).

### Page 7

Interestingly, corporate bonds attracted more foreign private capital than
Treasuries, underscoring the appeal of US corporate debt (chart).
III. US Economy On A Roll
The robust foreign demand for US financial assets is consistent with the
underlying strength of the US economy. The Atlanta Fed's GDPNow
model estimates Q3 real GDP growth at a solid 3.2% (saar). Consumer
spending is expected to grow at a robust 2.5% pace, while business
investment is projected to surge 9.2% (chart).

### Page 8

The labor market remains strong too, with initial jobless claims at just
203,000 and continuing claims at 1.76 million, both near multi-year lows
(chart).
IV. Gold Keeps Falling Through Support Levels
The gold market is experiencing a selling panic. The recent selloff below
$4,000 per ounce, down to $3,682, was accompanied by record increases
in daily volume in the GLD ETF (charts). We recently pushed our $5,000
target from the end of this year to the end of next year. Our conviction

level has been greatly reduced by the selloff since the start of the Middle
East war this year. We expect that the gold price will find support around
$3,400.

### Page 9

may be featured in the next "Just Ask Dr. Ed!"

## Document 312: Do Italian households have what it takes to finance Europe’s next ideas_ (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Do Italian households have what it takes to finance Europe’s next ideas_ (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

18 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
DO ITALIAN HOUSEHOLDS HAVE WHAT IT TAKES TO FINANCE EUROPE’S NEXT IDEAS?
Andrea d’Aquino
Between 2019 and 2025, US value added grew at twice the pace of Europe’s, driven largely by technology and labour productivity 1,2. Yet Europe
does not lack saving to finance a similar development: over the past ten years, Euro area gross national saving exceeded domestic investment
by about €300 bn a year, equivalent to 15% of productive investment 3, and in 2024-2025 gross capital outflows to the United States averaged

€785 billion a year 4. The situation described therefore creates a “European paradox”, where household particularly save but do not take on so
much risk to foster innovation as in the US.
Safety versus risk: how Italian and European households differ
In Q2 2026, Euro area households’ gross financial wealth grew by 6.5% y/y to approximately €37 trillion, of which just over 30% is held in
currency and deposits (€11,165 billion), compared with about 10% in the United States. Italy has the lowest share of currency and deposits

among the major Euro area economies (see Chart 1). In Q2 2026, Italian households’ financial wealth grew by 6.3% y/y, in line with the
Euro area, reaching €6,609 billion, of which 24.2% is held in currency and deposits (about €1,598 billion), compared with 28.3% in France
(€2,151 billion), 32% in Spain (€1,168 billion) and 36% in Germany (€3,660 billion).
1 Fabio Panetta; Il risparmio nella società che cambia, Banca d’Italia; 30th October 2026.
2 Luisa Carpinelli et al.; The Great Divide: productivity dynamics in the United States and euro area after the pandemic; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 924, Banca d’Italia; April

2025.
3 Productive investments are gross fixed investments minus residential construction.
4 It includes the item ‘other investments’, mainly made up of deposits, loans, and trade receivables.
SOURCE: EUROSTAT, BNL ECONOMIC RESEARCH
In Italy the largest share of households’ total financial assets is invested in equity
and investment fund shares, less in currency and deposits

Italian risk is tied up mostly in unlisted firms
Judging by this metric, European households’ risk appetite is lower than that of their US counterparts. Another metric to look at: the listed
shares. They account for 5% of Euro area gross financial wealth 5 (3.1% in Italy, 3.7% in France, 6.6% in Spain, 7.2% in Germany). However,
once holdings in unlisted companies are included, the share of risk capital rises to 15% in the Euro area, 34% in the United States and

more than 20% in Italy.
A large part of the gap reflects the wider presence in Europe of unlisted firms, largely family-owned. In Italy in particular, unlisted shares
account for 17.1% of gross financial wealth, just over 7 pp above the Euro area average, compared with 12.4% in France, 7.7% in Spain and
2.6% in Germany (see Chart 2). Unlisted family firms are on average smaller and less productive than the system as a whole, especially
among medium-sized and large firms and in technology- and knowledge-intensive sectors 6.

Italian households’ risk capital is therefore largely held in forms that are hard to diversify and in firms far from the technological frontier,
while the risk capital reaching the youngest firms (less than 12 years old) is scarce, even though these firms are very active in innovation:
innovative start-ups account for just over 2% of young firms but hold about 44% of that category’s patents. Similarly, venture-capital-
backed firms are barely 0.2% of young firms but account for around 12% of total patents of the youngest firms 7.

Italian inheritances will largely pass on bricks
Investment choices are also linked to demographics: risk appetite tends to rise until adulthood and then declines with advancing age 8.
According to Banca d’Italia 9, in the coming years in Italy, those born between 1966 and 1975 will benefit from wealth transfers to a greater
extent than earlier cohorts: inheritances would account for around 40% of their wealth, compared with 30% for those born between 1951

and 1965. All else being equal, households expecting to receive an inheritance hold more financial assets. What will change hands is
mainly real assets, above all housing: in 2022 (latest available data), households whose reference person was born between 1941 and
1950 held on average around 62% of their total wealth in dwellings, 28% in financial assets and almost 10% in equity stakes in businesses.
As a result, once households receive the inheritance, their portfolios could become more tilted towards housing, which may lead to a

rebalancing towards financial assets.
The challenge for the Euro area in the coming years will be to press ahead with the creation of a single market for capital, in order to
provide greater investment opportunities and avert the “European savings paradox”.
andrea.aquino@bnpparibas.com
5 The US data is not available for listed share only.
6 Michele Cascarano et al.; Le imprese familiari e i passaggi generazionali in Italia; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 1068, Banca d’Italia; October 2026.

7 Maria Giulia Cassinis et al.; Innovative firms unveiled: economic and financial insight from Italian start-ups; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 967, Banca d’Italia; October 2025.
8 Raffaella Giordano et al.; Population ageing and portfolio choices in the main EU countries; Questioni di Economia e Finanza n. 1036, Banca d’Italia; July 2026.
9 Relazione annuale sul 2025, Banca d’Italia; May 2026.

## Document 315: EcoNews of 23 November 2026 (2026-11-23)

Source PDF: all report pdf/EcoNews of 23 November 2026 (2026-11-23).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
5
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026ECONEWS
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
IN BRIEF
A week richer in geopolitical and market developments than macroe-
conomic ones:
19 November
While President Trump held warm meetings with Presidents Xi and
Putin in China (bilaterally and trilaterally, with no deliverables an -
nounced), 30+ country leaders gathered in Berlin at the Coalition of the

Willing Summit, hosted by German Chancellor Merz and co-chaired
with French President Macron and UK Prime Minister Burnham — to
pledge continued support to Ukraine, financial and otherwise.
20 November
The EU and UK had a successful Brexit reset summit yielding concrete
steps toward a closer economic relationship. Tech stocks led global
markets into a tailspin. The S&P 500 is down more than 10% from its
peak, and the Nasdaq more than 20%, implying negative returns year

to date. It lost $4tn of market capitalisation over the week, of which
half on Friday alone.
These moves, should they be sustained, let alone worsen, could call
into question the picture of economic strength and hawkish central
banks that has prevailed until now (including in the write-up below).
ADVANCED ECONOMIES
EUROZONE
Tame wage growth confirms rate outlook; new ECB leadership pac -
kage set. Negotiated wages rose 2.6% y/y in Q3. The labour cost index

grew faster (3.4%), but this partly reflects higher employment during
the period, which is a positive development. That said, ECB officials’
statements increasingly converged on the need for further tightening
as October inflation was confirmed at 4% y/y, and core at 2.6%. Ger -
many nominated Princeton academic Markus Brunnermeier to succeed
Philip Lane as ECB chief economist, while France nominated former Eu-
rope Minister Laurence Boone to succeed Isabel Schnabel. EU leaders

should formally endorse them, along with Pablo Hernández de Cos as
President, at their Summit next week. For a commentary on what this
means and the priorities that await them, see Isabelle Mateos y Lago’s
Substack and Editorial).
France
Mixed economic data, a surprise budget golden rule proposal, and
2027 electoral map starting to take shape. The INSEE business climate
index fell from 97 in October to 95 in November, with accommodation,
catering and retail particularly affected following the October riots.

Manufacturing proved more resilient, with its index at 104, above both
its 2026 and long-term average. Business creations were up 14% y/y
in October and 16.5% over the latest three months. Growth in bank
and market financing to businesses slowed respectively to 4.4% and
3.6% y/y in September (with the average cost of market financing ex -
ceeding that of bank financing by 60 bps, the most since December
2022).
On Sunday 22 November, President Macron surprised by announcing a

plan to convene the National Assembly and Senate in a special session
in early January, to vote on a constitutional reform initiated in 2011
that would require to balance the budget over time. This would require
a special majority of 3/5ths of the Congress, which arithmetically exists
based on parties that have recently proposed a fiscal rule. However,
success is far from assured given political dynamics. The passage of
this reform would go a long way to appease market fears over the

direction of French fiscal policy beyond the 2027 elections. Meanwhile,
Budget Minister Amiel said on Nov. 20 that France must find at least
€6 billion of additional savings in the 2027 budget after growth slum -
ped and the government made additional spending pledges. This is
consistent with the latest European Commission forecast that France's
budget deficit will reach 5.5% of GDP in 2026, exceeding the revised
5.4% target, and will barely narrow in 2027.

On the election front, with five months to go before the first round,
3 of the 4 leading candidates of the right and centre (David Lisnard,
Bruno Retailleau and Gabriel Attal) have opened discussions on a pos-
sible right-and-centre primary election at the end of January. Édouard
Philippe has ruled out taking part in it. Meanwhile, the National Rally
(Rassemblement national) has presented its long-awaited proposals
for pension reform. They include Marine Le Pen’s long-made promise

to lower the legal retirement age to 62, but with several measures
aimed at containing the costs of the reform. Also included is the crea -
tion of a mandatory capitalisation pillar. Despite these mitigating steps,
most analysts (ourselves included) expect the measures to have a net
negative impact on fiscal sustainability. For a synthetic primer on the
candidates’ fiscal and pension proposals, see our Focus.
Upcoming: INSEE household confidence (Wednesday); November HICP

inflation flash estimate, Q3 employment and final Q3 GDP (Friday).
Germany
Sentiment rebounds, but producer-price pressures accelerate shar -
ply. The ZEW economic confidence index jumped 7.5 points to 35.6 in
November, its highest since February 2026, while the assessment of
current conditions also improved, albeit remaining deeply negative.
Producer prices, meanwhile, surged 8.1% y/y and 1.4% m/m in October,
their fastest annual increase since February 2023, driven primarily by

energy and energy-intensive sectors.
Upcoming: November Ifo Business Survey (Tuesday); Q3 National Ac -
counts (Wednesday); November GfK Consumer Sentiment (Thursday).
Italy
Energy pushes inflation to a three-year high. Harmonised inflation
reached 5.6% y/y in October, the highest since September 2023, al -
though core inflation was considerably more contained, at 2.3%. Energy
inflation reached 31.8%, while goods prices rose 7.6% against 3.0% for

services.
UNITED KINGDOM
Consumers perk up even as inflation and labour-market strains
persist. Payroll employment fell by 34,000 in October, extending the
12-month decline to 95,000, and vacancies dropped to 690,000, their
lowest since 2014. The LFS measure was more encouraging, showing
employment up 156,000 in the three months to September and unem-
ployment edging down to 4.6%. Inflation accelerated slightly to 3.6%
y/y, while GfK consumer confidence recovered two points to -22 and

October retail-sales volumes rebounded 0.7% m/m after falling 0.6%
in September.
The Brexit reset inches forward. At a Summit on Friday hailed as a
reset of the UK-EU relationship, the two sides agreed on a series of
measures aimed at easing food trade, linking carbon markets and faci-
litating youth mobility. The implementation plan will also include coo-
peration on the EU’s “Made in Europe” initiative, potentially improving
UK firms’ access to EU procurement opportunities. UK Prime Minis -

ter Burnham is explicitly casting this agreement as the first step of a

### Page 2

6
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.43 / 23 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
Find out more in our scenario and forecasts
journey to reconsider Brexit. He intends to set out options that could
include rejoining the customs union or single market or possibly the
EU itself, although this is controversial even within his own party and
would in any case only happen in the next Parliament.
UNITED STATES
Resilient activity, struggling housing market and more Fed hikes

ahead. October headline activity data were soft, but the details held
up better. Industrial production fell 0.4% m/m, largely because of a
4.3% drop in utilities, while manufacturing output rose 0.3% m/m and
its annual growth reached 2.1%, the fastest since 2022. Retail sales
slowed to 0.2% m/m, but the GDP-relevant control group accelerated
to 0.6%. Housing remains the weak spot: starts rebounded 9.9% after
three months of sharp declines, but permits fell 7.5% and the NAHB

homebuilder index dropped to its lowest since 2012. Minutes from the
27-28 October FOMC meeting showed that “almost all participants”
expected further tightening under their baseline outlook and FOMC
members comments this week confirmed these views, albeit with disa-
greement over the pace and scale of extra tightening needed. We have
added one rate hike in 2027 to our earlier forecast, to take the Fed Fund
rate to 4.5-4.75% by mid-year.
Upcoming: Q3 GDP second estimate, Fed Beige Book, October PCE and

core PCE inflation, October durable-goods orders and new-home sales
(Wednesday).
JAPAN
Inflation moves further above target. Headline CPI rose 0.8% m/m and
2.3% y/y in October as energy prices increased for the first time since
July. Underlying pressures strengthened as the new core measure ex -
cluding fresh food and energy accelerated to 0.5% m/m. BoJ Governor
Kazuo Ueda expressed confidence that consumption would rebound
after disappointing Q3 GDP and reiterated that raising the policy rate

remained warranted.
TO WATCH NEXT WEEK
Major economies: Flash PMIs for November (Monday).
EMERGING ECONOMIES
ASIA
The AI-led equity market sell-off is spreading.
- Hong Kong Hang Seng Index: -5.2% between 13 November and 20 No-
vember;
- China CSI 300 Index: -1.5% between 13 November and 20 November
(of which IT subindex: -5.5%);
- South Korea Kospi Index: -8% between 13 November and 20 No -
vember and -30.1% since 1 October;
- Taiwan TWSE: -17.5% between 13 November and 20 November and

-25.5% since 1 October. The KRW and TWD have held up.
Southeast Asia is still one of the world’s strongest growth spots. Over
the first nine months of 2026, Vietnam led the region with 9% growth,
followed by Singapore at 6% and Malaysia at 5.6%, while Thailand and
the Philippines lagged with GDP growing at 2.8% and 2.5%. The diver -
gence largely reflects the stronger boost from AI-related investment
and semiconductor and electronics exports in the more technolo -

gy-oriented economies.
LATIN AMERICA
Brazil
Slower activity and fiscal doubts put the real under renewed pressure.
The central bank’s activity index contracted 0.9% q/q in Q3 amid tight
monetary conditions. The Senate approved a fiscal package expected to
improve the primary balance by 0.2pp of GDP, but concerns about the
public-debt trajectory persist. The real has depreciated 9% against the
dollar since President Lula’s re-election, prompting the central bank to

intervene in the foreign-exchange market on 19 November.
EMERGING EUROPE
Central Europe
Hungary : Policy rate on hold. As expected, the Central bank main -
tained its policy rate on hold at 5.5% for the 3 rd consecutive month.
Whilst inflation is still contained (2% y/y in October), it has been rising
and should pick up further in the coming months due to the end of fuel
price caps and the possible end of food margin caps. Meanwhile, the
future shift in the Central bank’s inflation target to 2.5% ±1% in 2028

and the need to anchor inflation expectations suggest some caution
ahead. In the meantime, Hungary’s 10-year sovereign bond yield has
fallen markedly since last April’s elections (-143 bp). In Poland, cur -
rency and bond markets may be more prone to volatility due to increa-
sing political tensions between the Central bank and the Constitutional
court.
Türkiye
Inflation fears and investment fund scandal shake sentiment. On No -
vember 12, the Turkish central bank raised its end-2027 inflation fo -

recast to 21% from 15%, citing the Iran war. Foreign currency deposits
held by Turkish residents rose $5.2 billion in the week through No -
vember 13 — the biggest weekly increase since July.
COMMODITIES
Oil and gas. Prices fall as markets look through geopolitical risks. Des-
pite a flare-up in attacks in the Strait of Hormuz and Saudi Arabia and
renewed rumours of imminent US-Israeli massive strikes on Iran, Brent
fell 0.5% over the week to $92/bbl as markets focused on the expected

year-end crude surplus forecast by the IEA, and improving refined-pro-
duct inventories. US commercial crude inventories increased another
3.4mb to 418mb, while SPR stocks fell 3.2mb. Diesel cracks declined
10% in the US and 8.5% in Europe. European gas prices rose by 11% to
€94/MWh amid forecasts of cold temperatures for the next two weeks.

## Document 330: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

Charts of the week: Oil’s disinflation effect

Falling oil and fuel prices likely to support disinflation in 2027; bonds are starting to offer greater diversification benefits 
Projected* y/y change in crude oil and gasoline prices by quarter     21- and 126-day rolling correlation of global equities and bonds

Source: US Energy Information Administration, Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *EIA projections

Editorial 
Staying diversified, adding US mega caps 
Strategy summary. AI-related concerns continue to weigh on 
equities, especially with new US restrictions on third-country 
access to advanced American semiconductor chips. Renewed 
volatility across the AI ecosystem drove the S&P500 volatility 
index (VIX) back above 25. However, two major event risks this 
week turned out to be less concerning - Nvidia’s Q3 revenue 
and earnings exceeded expectations, while Anthropic’s record 
initial public offering was priced above its indicative range.  
Our quant models are not signalling strong buy signal yet. 
However, with VIX spiking above 25 and investor positioning 
and sentiment indicators suggesting limited downside, we see 
value emerging in the US mega caps segment following its 
recent underperformance. We add an opportunistic idea on the 
segment, which we see benefitting from scale, financial 
strength and potential industry consolidation.  
Opportunistically adding US megacaps. Asian equities led 
declines this week, led by Taiwan, China and Korea, after new 
US export controls targeted access to advanced AI computing 
through overseas data centres. Licensing requirements focus 
on capacity supplied to restricted customers, with additional 
controls affecting regional hubs. These measures increase 
compliance burdens and financing uncertainty, potentially 
weighing on AI capital expenditure. We expect the market’s AI 
capex estimates for 2027 to be cut towards our own more-
conservative projections. Established cloud operators 
(hyperscalers) may be better equipped to manage customer 
checks than smaller providers. We initiate an opportunistic idea 
on US mega caps as we see a catch-up opportunity, given their 
established franchises, strong cash generation and relatively 
resilient earnings prospects (see page 7 for details). 
Diversifying beyond the AI trade. Although positive cross 
asset and sector correlations returned this week, sector 
rotations earlier this month reinforces the importance of 
maintaining exposure to different drivers of equity returns. Our 
preferred US sectors remain technology, healthcare, financials 
and materials, combining technology participation with 
opportunities beyond AI. Beyond the US, we like Euro area and 
Japanese banks, which are benefitting from elevated interest 
rates, actively managed global dividend strategies for income 
opportunities, and Indian equities due to their low correlation 
with the AI trade. 
Allure of high dividend equities. While global dividend yields 
remain historically low, attractive pockets persist. The Swiss 
equity market yields almost 4%, versus 1.6% for global equities. 
Euro area (3.6%), Australia (3.5%) and Singapore (3.4%) are 
other leading dividend paying markets. Taiwan offers almost a 
2% yield, combining income with structural growth 
opportunities. We favour active management over simply 
chasing yield, avoiding value traps. Dividend equities can 
complement bonds within a diversified portfolio, balancing 
income opportunities with broader exposure across markets. 
Increasing diversification offered by bonds. The one-month 
correlation between global stocks and bonds has turned 
negative after more than a year. This means bonds are offering 
greater diversification benefits amid the equity drawdown. 
Minutes from the Fed’s October meeting suggest policymakers 
are likely to hike rates further. However, we remain dovish vs. 
markets on the Fed’s 12-month rates outlook (we see Fed 
target rate at 4.25% by end-2027 vs. market pricing above 
4.5%) as lower oil prices and fading tariff effects lead to 
noticeable disinflation by H2 2027.  Thus, we see particular 
value in the 3-7-year maturity bucket, with a preference for BB-
to-BBB segment corporate bonds, which have resilient balance 
sheets and offer attractive yield pick-up. We see the US 10-year 
government bond offering value if the yield rises above 5.25%.  
Dollar appears overbought; potential for gold upside: The 
USD index (DXY) has jumped over 2% in the past two weeks 
after stronger-than-expected US jobs and inflation reports for 
October revived Fed rate hike bets. Nevertheless, crude oil 
prices have slumped almost 20% this month as the Republican 
party’s heavy losses in the US mid-term elections raised the 
prospects of de-escalation in the Middle East conflict. Forward 
markets are pricing US crude oil below USD 75/bbl by Q3 next 
year. Lower oil prices are likely to support disinflation next year. 
With DXY technically overbought and Fed policy expectations 
excessively hawkish, we expect a pullback in USD. Conversely, 
gold looks oversold. We see an upside for XAU/USD, despite 
revising lower our 3- and 12-month targets (see page 7).   
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions 
  How will the new US semiconductor restrictions impact 
the technology sector?  
Our view: While compute is likely to remain a bottleneck for AI, 
greater US regulatory restrictions could weigh on AI capex.   
Rationale: The US has announced broader restrictions on overseas 
computing capacity used by Chinese and other restricted-country 
companies, extending controls beyond physical chip shipments to 
remote access through data centres. This could delay data centre 
deployments and raise financing costs, as affected operators may 
defer expansion plans until licences or replacement customers are 
secured. Lower spending could weigh on chip and hardware orders, 
bringing consensus capex estimates closer to our forecasts. While 
we expect AI capex to rise 50% to USD 1.2trn in 2027, supported 
by projects already under construction, the compound annual growth 
rate (CAGR) between 2027 and 2030 is likely to moderate to c.12%.  
We therefore favour broadening technology exposure beyond 
semiconductors through our US Information Technology (IT) 
opportunistic idea, which also includes hardware and software 
industries. Investors should also consider diversifying into our other 
preferred sectors, such as US healthcare and financials.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  How were Chinese technology giants' Q3 2026 earnings? 
Our view: Chinese tech giants’ Q3 earnings highlight growing 
demand for AI, but heavy spending is pressuring margins and 
cash flow, underscoring the need for stronger AI monetisation. 
Rationale: Heavy AI capex continues to weigh on profitability, 
compressing near-term margins and deepening negative free cash 
flows, with share dilution a risk as tech giants may have to tap into 
equity markets for the AI buildout. Cloud growth points to genuine AI 
demand, but the AI-related revenue contribution remains small 
relative to the cost base. AI monetisation remains challenging, with 
competition set to intensify. Weak consumer spending was a 
common thread contributing to revenue misses. Major players 
reiterated the need for more tangible investment returns amid 
aggressive AI investments. 
In the near term, investor sentiment remains weighed down by 
tightening US restrictions targeting China’s access to advanced 
chips, introducing downside risks to 2027 AI cloud revenue. While 
this could hamper the development of next-generation AI models, 
accelerating domestic AI chip adoption should benefit local 
semiconductor companies as China pushes for technological self-
sufficiency. We expect rising AI adoption to benefit China's IT sector 
over time, though we prefer diversifying our China equity exposure 
across preferred sectors, such as financials and healthcare. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

AI capital expenditure (capex) should continue to 
rise, but growth is expected to moderate from 2028

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are the implications of the October Fed meeting 
minutes for the rate path forward and short-dated bonds? Do 
US 10-year government bonds now offer a good re-entry point? 
Our view: October’s Fed meeting minutes maintained a 
hawkish-hold narrative. We continue to expect the Fed to raise 
rates by 25bps at its 8-9 December meeting. However, with 
short-dated US government bonds having largely priced in 
three hikes by June 2027, we see any spike above 5.25% in the 
US 10-year bond yield as an opportunistic bullish entry point.  
Rationale: The hawkish-hold tone of the Fed’s October meeting 
minutes suggests the December Fed meeting is ‘live’. The market is 
currently pricing in three hikes by year-end 2027 (vs. our 
expectation of two hikes by H1 2027 followed by a potential cut 
in H2 2027). Short-dated government bond yields – using the US 2-
year yield as a proxy – have largely priced in three hikes, and we do 
not expect the hawkish minutes to have a material impact on them.   
Should the US 10-year government bond yield spike above 5.25%, 
the near-term risk-reward would become favourable, in our opinion. 
As we expect inflation to moderate in H2 2027, we see scope for 
the Fed to pivot towards easing, and we would turn more 
constructive on short-dated government bonds. We expect the US 
2-year government bond yield to decline to 4.00-4.25% in 12 
months, contributing to yield curve steepening. 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Is it time to add exposure to the EUR after its recent slide 
against the USD? 
Our view: Yes, we are bullish on EUR/USD and favour gradually 
adding EUR exposure in the coming weeks. Markets are pricing 
in more Fed tightening than we expect. Lower oil prices support 
the Euro area and are likely to ease the Fed’s inflation concerns 
on a forward-looking basis. 
Rationale: Lower oil prices are a double positive for EUR/USD, 
as they ease costs for Euro area households and businesses and 
temper US inflation, reducing the need for the Fed to tighten as 
much as markets expect.  
Markets are pricing in a terminal Fed funds rate close to 4.63%, down 
from 4.72% a week ago, with the yield curve now also pricing a rate-
cutting cycle after the September 2027 peak. We expect the bullish 
near-term USD momentum to linger until markets’ peak rate 
expectations converge towards our view. EUR/USD’s relative 
strength index (RSI) is below 30, and consolidation above 1.0950 
points to oversold conditions. We expect a rebound towards 1.11 
to confirm our recovery view in the next three weeks. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 20 November 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What could support a sustained recovery in the AUD? 
Our view: We believe a sustained AUD recovery requires a 
combination of domestic resilience and fading USD momentum. 
Rationale: AUD/USD has been pressured by two key external 
headwinds: hawkish Fed repricing and weaker AI-related risk 
sentiment. A reversal in either could provide scope for recovery. 
Softer US economic data or less hawkish Fed communication could 
ease upward pressure on US government bond yields, narrow rate 
differentials and weaken the USD. At the same time, a stabilisation 
in AI and broader equity sentiment could restore demand for the 
risk-sensitive AUD, particularly if Asian equities recover. 
Domestic fundamentals remain a relative source of support. 
Still-elevated inflation should constrain the RBA’s ability to turn 
dovish quickly, while exporter and real-money demand for the AUD 
could cushion the downside. However, Australia’s rising 
unemployment raises the prospect of RBA rate cuts in H2 2027, 
potentially eroding the AUD’s carry advantage over time. With 
AUD/USD technically oversold, we see scope for a rebound from 
0.6600, with 0.6830 as the key resistance level. 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  How will the improving oil supply-demand balance shape 
crude oil prices over the coming quarters? 
Our view: Improving supply-demand balances should support 
inventory rebuilding and ease oil prices over time, despite 
persistent geopolitical risks, supporting disinflation in H2 2027. 
Rationale: Crude oil’s recent retreat marks an important transition: 
expanding physical supply is finally asserting control over 
headline geopolitical noise. With non-OPEC production resilient, 
Gulf exports recovering and alternative transit routes adapting, 
markets are steadily pricing in an approaching supply surplus. 
However, high freight costs and extended voyage times keep 
significant crude oil volumes tied up at sea rather than refilling local 
storage tanks. Crucially, the US Strategic Petroleum Reserve’s 
crude inventories have fallen nearly 33% from pre-conflict levels, 
leaving a thinner buffer against renewed supply disruptions. 
For investors, improving fundamentals point to gradual price 
normalisation as trade routes adapt, despite persistent geopolitical 
volatility. We expect near-term West Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil 
to range between USD 80/bbl and USD 90/bbl. Softer energy 
prices should provide some relief to headline inflation, but are 
unlikely to materially alter the Fed’s near-term policy trajectory. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

Top client questions 
  Are there any tactical opportunities amidst the equity 
drawdown? 
Our view: We introduce US mega cap equities as a new 
opportunistic idea, favouring their scale and financial strength 
amid heightened volatility and potential industry consolidation. 
Rationale: We believe the mid-cycle backdrop favours large 
companies with strong cash generation, established competitive 
positions and the financial capacity to invest through periods of 
uncertainty. US mega caps’ forward P/E has fallen well below its 
two-year average and the -2x standard deviation level, offering a 
more attractive entry point into US market leaders. 
A potential reset in AI capex expectations could also accelerate 
industry consolidation, favouring established leaders with the 
balance sheets, infrastructure and customer reach to sustain 
investment and capture market share. Key risks include a sharper-
than-expected economic slowdown, weaker returns on AI 
investment, regulatory pressures, key man risks and concentration 
in a few dominant companies.  
—  Robin Xie, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Do you see opportunities in gold after the recent sell-off? 
Our view: We are lowering our gold price targets to USD 
4,000/oz (three months) and USD 4,500/oz (12 months). Despite 
gold’s recent drop below our revised three-month price target, 
we continue to believe the precious metal can rebound in the 
medium term. 
Rationale: Several factors have been driving down gold prices. A 
combination of stronger-than-expected US jobs, consumer 
inflation and producer inflation reports since early November has 
reinforced the case for Fed tightening. This, in turn, brought renewed 
hawkish Fed rhetoric, driving up the repricing of Fed rate-hike 
expectations. Higher rates raise the opportunity cost of holding non-
yielding gold, while also strengthening the USD – a double 
headwind for bullion. In addition, recent volatility in the equity 
markets has raised liquidity needs for leveraged investors, causing 
some of them to sell gold to cover their losses.  
However, at current levels, gold has undershot even our newly 
revised three-month price target for the precious metal. Emerging 
Market central banks remain structural buyers of gold as they 
continue to shift away from USD-denominated reserves. The US 
fiscal outlook remains dire, and with political gridlock now having 
returned to Washington, persistent fiscal deficits with no reforms will 
likely remain entrenched for the next two years at least. Confidence 
in US government finances and the USD is expected to weaken 
into 2027, and investors are likely to once again turn to gold as a 
store of value, informing our 12-month price target of USD 4,500/oz. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income and FX
